FULL CURRENT COMMITTED FILE @S@reference/documentation-governance.md
# Documentation Governance

Status: current maintenance contract
Audience: documentation contributor, maintainer, reviewer
Owner: documentation
Evidence: scripts/docs-governance.mjs; scripts/docs-publication.mjs; scripts/docs-status.mjs; docs/status/open-issues.json
Applies to: all reader-facing files below docs/site
Last verified: source-bound maintenance revision; publication and reader results remain separate

## Purpose

This page explains which document owns each type of information. It also explains how to keep that information current.

A link can work while its statement is false. A generated table can be current while its explanation is unclear.
For these reasons, KubeClaw checks source facts and authored explanations through separate paths.

## One Canonical Home for Each Topic

Use the following homes. Link to a canonical page when another reader path needs the same explanation.
Do not copy the explanation into a second page.

| Information | Canonical home | Owner role |
| --- | --- | --- |
| System behavior and component boundaries | `understand/` | The named component or platform owner |
| Operator procedures and recovery | `use/` | Platform operations |
| Extension contracts and development tasks | `extend/` | Plugin foundation or the named specialist owner |
| Exact generated facts and shared terms | `reference/` | Documentation and the source component owner |
| Enduring choices and their reasons | `decisions/` | Platform architecture or the named decision owner |
| Current implementation limits | `status/current.md` and `status/open-issues.md` | Platform architecture and platform maintainers |
| Planned work | `status/roadmap.md` | Platform maintainers |
| Environment acceptance | `status/acceptance.md` | Platform operations |

A page can link to several areas. It must not become a second authority for their facts.
For example, an operator procedure can summarize a trust boundary and link to its full architecture explanation.

## Source Authority

Use the narrowest source that owns a fact.

1. A schema owns accepted fields, types, required values, and structural limits.
2. A runtime manifest owns package membership for that role.
3. Implementation source owns current behavior.
4. A focused test proves only the behavior that it executes.
5. A decision record owns an approved reason or constraint.
6. The open-issue register owns incomplete implementation work.
7. A live acceptance record owns results from a real environment.

Do not use code as proof of approval. Do not use an accepted decision as proof of implementation.
Do not use a local test as proof of deployment behavior.

The publication tool checks page metadata and repository evidence paths. See the
[metadata check](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/9509581d0e7bacfe36e4edf63618802753abbcd2/scripts/docs-publication.mjs#L831-L861).
The status generator reads the machine-owned issue register. See the
[status source boundary](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/9509581d0e7bacfe36e4edf63618802753abbcd2/scripts/docs-status.mjs#L113-L159).

## Generator prerequisites

Run documentation generators from the repository root with Node.js 24 and
installed repository dependencies. API output discovery requires Helm
3.22.0 and Go 1.26.3 on `PATH`. Helm renders local charts against the declared
Kubernetes 1.35.0 API baseline; rendering does not deploy them. The Go helper
uses the standard parser to inspect the controller's source. It does not build
or run the controller. The documentation workflow
pins the same Helm and Go versions for both validation and trusted regeneration.
Use `node --version`, `helm version --short`, and `go version` to confirm the tools before generation.
If a required tool is absent, install it through the development environment's
approved toolchain procedure. A missing parser is a failed prerequisite, not
permission to omit controller-produced API objects from the inventory.

## Authored Explanations and Generated Facts

Authored text explains purpose, sequence, reasons, consequences, failure, and recovery.
A maintainer reviews this text when a source dependency changes.

Generated content lists repetitive facts from manifests, schemas, or registers.
Do not edit generated content by hand. Change its source or generator, and then regenerate it.

Use the [generated page map](generated-documentation-map.json) to locate each
page's declared owner, content kind, source dependencies, and checks. Do not
maintain a second manual inventory here. The governance generator builds the map from reader-page
metadata; it does not prove complete source discovery or successful checks.
A missing or stale page entry must be corrected through its metadata and the
map generator before the change is called covered.

The plugin catalogue has two input authorities. Source manifests and schemas
own mechanical package facts. Maintainers own the purpose, use, exclusions, and
limits in the catalogue guidance input. The generator reads that guidance; do
not edit its generated package pages as a second authored authority. See the
[current page construction](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/9509581d0e7bacfe36e4edf63618802753abbcd2/scripts/docs-publication.mjs#L255-L299).
The plugin-foundation owner maintains package guidance. Documentation and the
source component owner maintain the generated-fact route. For other families,
use the page's named owner and the
[maintenance workflow](../extend/documentation-maintenance.md#choose-one-fact-authority-and-its-explanation).

## Required Page Metadata

Every Markdown page has six fields directly below its title.

| Field | Meaning | Update trigger |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `Status` | The page's present claim, not a completion slogan | Its implementation or decision state changes |
| `Audience` | The reader roles that need the page | A supported task or reader path changes |
| `Owner` | The role that reviews and maintains the page | Component responsibility changes |
| `Evidence` | Repository sources that support the page | A source moves, splits, or loses authority |
| `Applies to` | The versions, components, or scope of the claim | Compatibility or scope changes |
| `Last verified` | The last source or environment review and its limit | The page receives a new applicable check |

The generated [documentation map](generated-documentation-map.json) records these fields for every page.
It also assigns the checks that protect each page. The generator fails if any page lacks required metadata.

Run these commands after a documentation change:

```bash
npm run docs:governance:generate
npm run docs:governance:check
npm run docs:publish-check
```

The first command updates the map. The second command detects drift without changing files.
The final command runs the complete local publication gate.

## Decision Records

A stable decision identifier never changes its meaning. A decision record includes these items when evidence exists:

- the problem and its constraints;
- the selected approach and its scope;
- alternatives that the source records;
- the reason for the choice;
- benefits, costs, and operational consequences;
- approval status and provenance;
- implementation and verification status; and
- its successor or related decisions.

If historical evidence does not name an alternative, date, or approver, say that it is unknown.
Do not invent history to make the record look complete.

An accepted decision can have partial implementation. A replaced decision remains in history and points to its successor.
The successor states which part it replaces. It also states which earlier guarantees remain valid.

## Deprecation, Redirect, and Supersession

Use these rules before changing a published route or term:

1. Select the successor page or term.
2. State why the old item is no longer correct.
3. State the compatibility period when one exists.
4. Add a redirect before removing a published route.
5. Link the old decision to its successor when historical meaning matters.
6. Remove an alias only after its declared compatibility period ends.

The repository has no earlier public-route manifest. Therefore, this documentation does not invent redirects for unknown historical URLs.
The [route registry](documentation-route-registry.json) records declared redirects and deprecated terms.
The generated map establishes the current route baseline. Future route changes must preserve declared redirects in the registry.

A repository source path is not automatically a published route. Moving an internal record does not create a public redirect requirement.

## Definition of Complete

A page is not complete because it exists or because a build passes. Completion requires all applicable results below:

- claims agree with their source and declared revision;
- mechanisms, ownership, failures, recovery, and limits have sufficient detail;
- significant choices include reasons and consequences;
- a new technical reader can follow the explanation without chat history;
- instructions contain prerequisites, observations, stop conditions, and recovery;
- extension tasks produce the declared checked result;
- links and evidence resolve in source and publication output;
- an owner, source dependency, and check protect the page; and
- source, local-test, deployment, and human-acceptance states remain distinct.

The project controlled-language review applies ASD-STE100 principles. Formal
ASD-STE100 verification or certification is not an acceptance gate and is not
claimed by this documentation.

## Change Procedure

1. Identify the canonical page and its owner in the generated map.
2. Read each listed source dependency before changing a claim.
3. Update authored explanation and generated facts through their separate paths.
4. Update decision and status pages when authority or implementation state changes.
5. Refresh `Last verified` with the actual check scope.
6. Generate the documentation map.
7. Run the mapped checks and the complete publication gate.
8. Record checks that need a live environment as pending.

Do not change `Last verified` after a formatting-only edit. Do not claim a live result from static implementation evidence.

FULL CURRENT COMMITTED FILE @S@extend/documentation-maintenance.md
# Maintain documentation from source changes

Status: source-described maintainer workflow; each change needs its own check and reader results
Audience: developer, documentation maintainer, documentation agent
Owner: documentation
Evidence: scripts/docs-governance.mjs; scripts/docs-generate.mjs; scripts/docs-inventory.mjs; scripts/docs-configuration-inventory.mjs; scripts/check-configuration-cli-drift.mjs; scripts/check-configuration-drift-mutations.mjs; scripts/docs-platform-surface-inventory.mjs; scripts/docs-publication.mjs; scripts/docs-tree-boundary.mjs; scripts/docs-parity-transition.mjs; docs/config/documentation-tree-classification.json; docs/config/documentation-tree-baseline.json; @S@reference/documentation-route-registry.json; package.json; .github/workflows/docs-checks.yaml
Applies to: authored reader pages, source-derived documentation, and their ownership and publication inputs
Last verified: implementation behavior checked on 2026-10-15; no generation, check, mutation test, or reader exercise asserted

## Start with the changed behavior

A nested configuration field changes its default. Updating a table is not enough:
the consumer may apply a different default, an operator may need a restart, and
an old example may now request the wrong behavior. This workflow connects the
source change to its explanation, generated facts, failure path, and reader task.
Use it for a new surface, a changed meaning, and a removal. A documentation agent
uses the same steps and stop conditions as a human maintainer.

Use the [Developer handbook](developer-handbook.md#route-each-source-change-to-its-documentation-owner)
for component-specific owners and checks. Use [documentation governance](../reference/documentation-governance.md)
for canonical homes and source authority. This page joins those rules into one
change procedure; it does not replace the component's technical guide.

## Prepare a bounded change

1. Start from a checkout whose revision and existing changes you can identify.
   Record the changed source paths, selected tools, dependency state, and intended
   outputs. Preserve unrelated work. Use the handbook's
   [checkout and component preparation](developer-handbook.md) before running tools.
2. Name the reader's question and the behavior owner. Distinguish product source,
   a supported dependency configuration, a generated fact, and explanatory prose.
   A schema declaration alone does not prove a consumer inserts its default.
3. Open the [page map](../reference/generated-documentation-map.json). Locate the
   source dependency or parent component and its owner, page, and declared checks.
   Read the actual generator's boundary and the actual consumer. If no mapping
   exists, assign an owner and canonical page before calling the change covered.
4. Identify permissions and effects before execution. Node.js 24 and locked root
   dependencies are common prerequisites, not the whole toolchain. Follow the
   [selected-route tools](developer-handbook.md#add-tools-and-credentials-only-for-the-selected-route)
   for any Helm, Go, Kubernetes, native, or other requirement. A source or local
   check is not a live deployment result. Missing prerequisites stop that route;
   do not switch to a partial parser or omit a source to obtain a green result.

Keep a small change record: source boundary, parser/selector, owner, canonical
page, generator or manual explanation, check, reader task, and exclusions.
This record is a work aid. Registering it does not prove discovery complete.
The generated page map lists metadata dependencies and check names, but does not
itself inventory every public surface, run a reader task, or choose every test.

## Discover the whole affected family

Search the source boundary before selecting a convenient example. Include
conditional and disabled supported branches, shared helpers, generated names,
and callers that change defaults or authority. Read the actual extractor's
roots, exclusions, and supported syntax. An unsupported source form is a blocker
for that extractor, not proof that the surface does not exist.

| Family | Facts to preserve with a source location | Reader destination and bounded tool route |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Schema and configuration | Recursive objects, arrays, alternatives, conditions, required relationships, constraints, schema defaults versus runtime defaults, deprecated fields, consumer, precedence, sensitive data, reload/new-run effects | Start at [Configuration precedence](../reference/configuration-precedence.md), the selected family page, and [Plugin configuration](../reference/plugin-configuration.md). `docs:inventory:config` derives its declared YAML/schema/runtime-input inventories; inspect its boundary before treating them as global coverage. |
| Commands and flags | Owning entrypoint, subcommand, aliases, value-taking, required/optional relationships, defaults, duplicate and unknown-option behavior, exit/failure meaning, consumer | Use [CLI commands](../reference/cli.md) and the component guide. `docs:inventory` covers deployment/secret/Helm/workflow facts; the configuration runtime-input inventory has a separate owned-definition CLI surface. `docs:drift:cli:check` checks that inventory and its declared semantics. External-command invocations are not owned CLI definitions. |
| Contract | Exact identity/version, producer and consumer, schema/type relationship, compatibility direction, migration and retirement | Use [Configuration compatibility](../reference/configuration-compatibility.md), [SDK](../reference/sdk.md), or the component's contract guide. Select its real generator/check; do not assume a configuration-schema extractor covers all request, result, and snapshot contracts. |
| Event | Producer, consumer or explicit unknown consumer, payload/version, transport, correlation, ordering, repeat delivery, persistence and retention | Use [Telemetry](../understand/telemetry.md) and the component guide. The [platform surface reference](../reference/platform-surfaces-generated.md) supplies bounded facts, not every payload's meaning. Read its discovery boundary. |
| Error | Exact code or dynamic construction rule, throw/emit location, trigger, effect and lifecycle state, retry rule, safe action, retained data | Use [Configuration errors](../reference/configuration-errors.md), [Buster errors](../reference/buster-error-codes.md), and the producing component. Follow dynamic prefixes/arguments and shared error constructors; a string-literal search alone cannot establish completeness. |
| Endpoint | Producer/listener, caller, Service/route/port, transport, authentication and authority, limits, failure and recovery | Use [Endpoints](../reference/endpoints.md) and [Service routes](../reference/service-routing.md). Reconcile chart/config/runtime consumers, not only one default render. |
| Store | Writer/reader, exact path or resolution rule, authority, lifetime, retention, quota, cleanup, backup/restore and loss impact | Use [Data and state](../understand/data-and-state.md), [Recovery](../use/recovery.md), and [Capacity](../use/capacity.md). An absent default can defer to another authority; do not invent a retention policy or restore command. |
| Capability | Namespace, provider, caller grant, resource match, translation boundary, denial behavior | Use [Capabilities](../reference/capabilities.md) and [Roles and package ownership](../reference/roles-and-package-ownership.md). A runtime role, Nova grant, Buster capability, and Kubernetes RBAC rule are not interchangeable. |
| Workflow | Trigger, owner, inputs/secrets, permissions, tools, outputs, external effects, success/failure and rollback limits | Use [Workflow inventory](../reference/workflows.md) and the task procedure. `docs:inventory` derives declared workflow facts; an inventory of commands does not prove that they ran. |

For each affected family, compare discovered identities to documented identities
in both directions. Find a source with no page and a page with no current source.
Keep exclusions and unknown semantics visible. Do not extrapolate a fixed count,
one passing example, or one component's extractor to the whole product.
The product boundary includes KubeClaw-owned interfaces and supported dependency
choices; it does not require copying every unused upstream Kubernetes field.
Link applicable upstream definitions while explaining the choice KubeClaw makes.

## Choose one fact authority and its explanation

Put a mechanical fact in its source and let its owning generator derive it.
Write the reason, operational consequence, compatibility, and safe recovery in
the authored page. Do not manually maintain a second list of generated fields.

| Change | Update together | Do not substitute |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Nested field/default | Consumer/schema relationship, recursive reference, effective-value explanation, example, reload/change impact, rejection case | A schema default for evidence that runtime inserts it |
| New flag | Actual parser, owner/subcommand, help/default/precedence/duplicate semantics, guide, negative input | A shell invocation of another tool for a new product option |
| Changed error | Producer and dynamic-family rule, exact reference, lifecycle/retry/safe action, positive and negative case | A renamed label while keeping the old recovery instruction |
| New event or store | Producer/consumer or writer/reader, version/path resolution, authority, retention/failure explanation | A generated name list for operational meaning |
| New reader page | Six metadata fields, canonical classification, incoming task link, evidence paths, generated map inputs | A hand-written entry in the generated map |

Use Status, Audience, Owner, Evidence, Applies to, and Last verified on every
reader page. Evidence paths are repository-relative and separated by semicolons.
Do not advance Last verified beyond the actual source or execution evidence.
Use a full committed revision and valid narrow line range for explicit pinned
source links. Compare the cited bytes to the current source; an unchanged old
link can point to behavior that no longer applies. Keep uncommitted source
provenance explicit until the publication revision can genuinely contain it.

A new page under `docs/site` also needs one explicit canonical record in
`docs/config/documentation-tree-classification.json`: matching actual and
expected path, canonical-reader class, purpose, original path, and the correct
introduced-after-baseline flag. Preserve the immutable baseline; do not recapture
it to conceal a new or moved file. The generic tree tooling and publication
selector must agree. Non-Markdown reader assets need their separate allowance.

## Generate only owned facts, then check

Follow the handbook's [single common chain](developer-handbook.md#documentation-change-chain).
The following choices explain its effects; they do not authorize a command in
an environment whose source/tool prerequisites have not been qualified.

| Input change | Selected command and output | Check or failure meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Page metadata or evidence dependencies | `npm run docs:governance:generate` writes `@S@reference/generated-documentation-map.json` | `docs:governance:check` compares expected bytes and validates route metadata. Missing owner/evidence needs source metadata repair, not a map patch. |
| Deployment/secret setup/Helm/workflow source facts | `npm run docs:inventory` writes its four inventories under `docs/generated/inventory` | `docs:inventory:check` compares those expected JSON bytes. This does not cover every runtime input. |
| Declared configuration source families | `npm run docs:inventory:config` writes values, schema, and runtime-input JSON inventories; then selected reference generation | `docs:inventory:config:check` rejects stale bytes and verifies external Helm authorities on its successful-current path. Full discovery may acquire tools/children; it is not just a JSON comparison. |
| Source-derived reference sections | `npm run docs:generate` updates its owned reference outputs | `docs:generate:check` detects mismatch. Prepare the actual source revision and selected discovery route; do not suppress unsupported inputs. |
| Plugin catalogue/capability/operator matrix | `npm run docs:publication:generate` writes catalogue/capability pages and the marked operator matrix | `docs:publication:check` first runs helper tests and then compares/checks publication inputs. It does not validate every product example or live effect. |
| New/moved documentation inputs | `npm run docs:tree:generate` derives the tree inventory from classification, baseline, current inputs, and consumers | `docs:tree:check` rebuilds and compares the model. Its dependency/consumer analysis has prerequisites; do not infer a pure file read from its name. |

> **Source — ownership records and generator selection**
>
> Claim: page metadata supplies ownership/dependencies; generators select their owned outputs; check modes compare the derived result instead of making an authored page authoritative product input.
> Implementation: `scripts/docs-governance.mjs`, `pages` metadata and generator selection, lines 85–108; output/check/generate branch, lines 124–143. `scripts/docs-generate.mjs`, `stableWriteMap`, lines 131–154, selects reference outputs. `scripts/docs-tree-boundary.mjs`, `main`, lines 904–936, selects byte comparison or tree output writing.
> Contract: the six metadata fields and source dependencies feed the generated page map; classification and the immutable baseline feed the tree model. A map row or selected output is not evidence of complete source discovery.
> Validation: no generator, checker, mutation test, or reader task execution for this statement.
> Checkout identity, SHA-256: governance `a4a44fdb62fb8a963e1e7aa84fdacf41edfab06282e31938bda412252ea18502`; reference generator `c83c5653177b9b0a56326b0e315c409dc3c814c95d37dd53933402a2288aa718`; tree checker `a432a90ab447e4f9b944fae829aca7841eaad61493bc59fba629d7d2c3610a92`. These identify current file bytes, not claimed committed revisions.
> Limit: selected SDK/discovery, source/Git and child-process prerequisites remain in force. The locators do not admit those effects or prove any generated result current.

Inspect every generated diff and require exit zero before the next step.
Generators can write earlier files before a later failure. Retain the first
error, partial diff, selected revision and tool versions; fix the input or tool
cause and rerun that same owned generator. Do not use a broad reset, edit generated
facts manually, or pass a gap-allowance switch to hide a production omission.
For prose-only changes, avoid unrelated generator writes.

The common stable sequence is:

```sh
set -eu
npm run docs:governance:check
npm run docs:generate:check
npm run docs:check:refs
npm run docs:site-boundary:check
npm run docs:publication:check
```

Run the changed component's named checks before publication checking. Keep the
actual command, exit status, cases passed/failed/skipped, tool versions, source
revision, and changed-file observations. Stop on the first failed prerequisite
or required check; do not average a failed boundary into other successes.
A command listed in the map is a requirement to evaluate, not a saved result.
The broader `docs:check` and `docs:publish-check` include additional tests and
staging effects. Use [Documentation site and publication](../reference/documentation-site.md)
to inspect their output and distinguish Markdown staging from rendered behavior.

## Prove the changed meaning, not a fixed count

Choose the smallest complete checks for the affected source and consumer.
For a nested default change, verify the actual absent-input result, an explicit
accepted value, an invalid value, and any conditional branch that changes the
default or authority. For a removed error identity, verify its absence from
current output, the retained dynamic family, and the changed safe-action text.
Do not mutate production source as an experiment while another task uses it.

Use isolated fixtures for addition, semantic change, and removal in each affected
reference family. First demonstrate a healthy baseline. Introduce one mutation,
require the intended drift failure and named affected page, restore the fixture,
and require recovery. A generic parser crash or unrelated stale file is not the
intended rejection. New-source and passive-neighbor cases distinguish discovery
from a hard-coded expected count. Preserve current bytes and the fixture diff.
The existing configuration mutation suite creates a temporary tree, copies
inputs, and starts generator/publisher child processes; it also queries Git.
It is not a no-write check. Inspect its current scope and dependencies before
selecting it, and do not apply its results to untested error/event/store families.

Validate examples against their actual contract, not merely JSON syntax.
Record the schema/version, consumer, setup, expected output, failure/cleanup,
and any external effects. Execute only in the documented isolated or authorized
environment. The publication checker has selected plugin and resume-signal
schema examples; that does not validate every operator or infrastructure command.
A compiled example is not evidence of activation, recovery, or a live service.

Ask independent readers to use the page without chat history or undocumented
maintainer hints. Give a concrete Find, Understand, and Act task chosen before
they read it. For the default example: find the field, explain which consumer
wins, identify restart/new-run impact, choose the valid override and safe
rejection response. Retain missing steps as defects. Independently check source
accuracy and language/navigation quality; the author cannot supply all of those
independent observations by rereading the same draft.

## Rename, retire, and recover

Before removing a page or source identity, account for every still-valid claim,
consumer, generated row, example, incoming link, owner, and historical route.
Move required information into its canonical successor and verify parity before
deleting the old authority. Use the [route registry](../reference/documentation-route-registry.json)
and [governance](../reference/documentation-governance.md) for a genuine public
rename, not for a fabricated former URL. Preserve fragment targets or explicitly
migrate them. The registry's structural validation does not prove server or
static redirects; test real old/new requests and anchors in the selected renderer.
The repository's transition tooling has its own manifests, source snapshots,
child checks and effects. Read that contract before a destructive transition;
an unrelated prose change does not require or permit a retirement run.

| Failure | Stop and retain | Safe next action |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Source revision differs from current evidence | Source bytes, selected revision, first diagnostic | Complete the handbook's source-revision procedure; do not weaken a pin or cite an old commit as current. |
| New identity lacks semantics or owner | The discovered source and unmapped identity | Assign its real consumer/owner and explanation; keep its missing coverage explicit. |
| Generator failed after writing | Exit status, first error, owned partial diff | Repair prerequisites/source, rerun only the responsible generator, and inspect unrelated work remains intact. |
| Check reports stale output | Exact path, expected owning generator, input revision | Repair input and regenerate that output. Never hand-edit it merely to satisfy equality. |
| Reader cannot finish a documented step | Task, failed step, missing prerequisite or unsafe ambiguity | Correct the guide and repeat the affected task with an independent reader. |
| Rename loses route/fragment or required meaning | Old link and successor mapping, parity defect | Restore the needed authority or fix successor/redirect before removal. |
| Local checks pass but live route is unavailable | Actual local results and the missing environment/prerequisite | Keep live behavior unverified; do not claim deployment, recovery, or retirement complete. |

Finish by reconciling source, authored explanation, generated facts, incoming
navigation, revision links, and the actual check/reader outcomes. Keep unresolved
families and live limitations visible. Publication is ready only for the scope
actually demonstrated; a complete-looking table is not proof of completeness.

# Plugin SDK reference

Status: current SDK API reference
Audience: plugin author, runtime maintainer
Owner: SDK maintainers (plugin-foundation)
Evidence: skills/common/plugin-runtime/sdk/package.json; skills/common/plugin-runtime/sdk/src/index.ts; skills/common/plugin-runtime/sdk/src/values.ts; skills/common/plugin-runtime/sdk/src/runtime.ts; skills/common/plugin-runtime/sdk/src/source-revision.ts; skills/common/plugin-runtime/sdk/src/review-subject.ts; skills/common/plugin-runtime/sdk/src/source-preflight.ts; skills/common/plugin-runtime/sdk/src/source-approval.ts; skills/common/plugin-runtime/contracts/plugin-system/v2/plugin-system-v2.schema.json; scripts/docs-sdk-semantics.mjs
Applies to: current private workspace SDK entrypoints and plugin-system v2 contracts
Last verified: 2026-10-12; source and bounded pure JSON checks

The plugin SDK is the shared contract between a pipeline plugin and its runtime. Use it to type a stage, observer, capability adapter, test provider or report adapter. Use its helpers to preserve exact JSON bytes and source identity. The SDK does not activate a package, issue a capability grant, start an agent or provide a database.

A **capability** is a named operation that the runtime permits a plugin to request. A **lease** is the current, revocable authority for one invocation. An **artifact** is stored output with an exact byte digest and producer identity. A **digest** is a SHA-256 hash of selected bytes; equal digests do not prove equal authority.

The SDK maintainers (plugin-foundation) own this authored explanation and review its product-contract claims. Documentation automation owns the separate generated export inventory. Evidence: source-checked against `586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1`. The bounded JSON unit check is listed below. Examples that call capabilities are source-only; no activation, provider process, external effect or live deployment is claimed.

## Find the contract you need

The [complete export and field inventory](sdk-exports.md) gives every exported type, helper, constant and testing function. Each entry has its consumer meaning, compiler-discovered shape, source, schema constraints where applicable, and a source-only example. Read that entry before relying on an optional field or an unfamiliar helper.

| Task | Entry or section |
| --- | --- |
| Implement a stage or observer | [`PluginInvocationContext`](sdk-exports.md#plugininvocationcontext), [`StageResult`](sdk-exports.md#stageresult), [`ObserverHandler`](sdk-exports.md#observerhandler) |
| Implement a capability adapter | [`AdapterFactory`](sdk-exports.md#adapterfactory), [`AdapterInstance`](sdk-exports.md#adapterinstance), [`AdapterInvocation`](sdk-exports.md#adapterinvocation), [`AdapterActivationContext`](sdk-exports.md#adapteractivationcontext) |
| Implement a test provider | [`TestProviderFactory`](sdk-exports.md#testproviderfactory), [`TestProviderInstance`](sdk-exports.md#testproviderinstance), [`TestProviderExecutionContext`](sdk-exports.md#testproviderexecutioncontext) |
| Parse a test report | [`ReportAdapterFunction`](sdk-exports.md#reportadapterfunction), [`ReportAdapterInput`](sdk-exports.md#reportadapterinput), [`ReportAdapterOutput`](sdk-exports.md#reportadapteroutput) |
| Serialize or hash data | [JSON and digest rules](#json-and-digest-rules) |
| Read exact artifact bytes | [Artifact-byte verification](#artifact-byte-verification) |
| Select source, preflight and approval | [Source selection and approval](#source-selection-and-approval) |
| Preserve an old encoding | [Version and absence rules](#version-and-absence-rules) |
| Add a testing exhaustiveness check | [Testing entrypoint](#testing-entrypoint) |

For package layout, manifest registration, activation and disablement, use [extension contracts](../extend/contracts.md) and the [Extend entry](../extend/README.md). This page owns SDK API behavior; those pages own the extension task. The [developer handbook](../extend/developer-handbook.md) explains the contributor change/check route.

## Package and import boundary

`@kubeclaw/plugin-sdk` is a private workspace package. Its package manifest exports exactly `.` and `./testing`. The main entrypoint exports contracts, values, runtime helpers, source helpers and selected runtime interfaces. The testing entrypoint exports `assertNever` only. Do not assume that a source file is a supported package subpath.

```ts
import {
  PLUGIN_API_VERSION,
  portableJson,
  sha256Text,
  type PluginInvocationContext,
  type StageResult,
} from '@kubeclaw/plugin-sdk';
import { assertNever } from '@kubeclaw/plugin-sdk/testing';
```

The package points to TypeScript source in this workspace. Its build configuration emits declarations, not a separate JavaScript SDK implementation. Use the repository's supported Node/TypeScript setup; do not assume this private package is published for installation from a registry.

`PLUGIN_API_VERSION` is `pipeline-plugin-v2`. It is the manifest API token, not a package version, transport encoding or artifact version. `ProviderInvocationV1`, `ProviderResultV1`, `ReportAdapterResultV1`, `ReportCountsV1`, `ReportCaseV1` and `FindingV1` belong to `@kubeclaw/pipeline-test-gate-contract`. SDK callback signatures refer to them, but the SDK does not re-export them as named types.

The public `CapabilityInvocation` is the readonly interface selected by the main entrypoint's explicit type export. A generated contract with the same name is not the public named shape. `AdapterInvocation` reaches the fenced/confidential variants and `AdapterResourceFence`, but those three support declarations are not named exports from the main entrypoint. Use the union and its discriminator instead of importing an internal support type. `PluginSystemV2.capabilityInvocation` refers to the generated, mutable `CapabilityInvocation` with a string index of `any`, not the same-named public readonly runtime interface. The [qualified support closure](sdk-exports.md#reachable-support-types) preserves that separate declaration and all reachable KubeClaw callback types without adding named SDK exports.

| Source field | Evidence |
| --- | --- |
| Claim | The manifest selects two TypeScript entrypoints; explicit runtime type exports determine the public invocation shape. |
| Implementation | [Main exports](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/common/plugin-runtime/sdk/src/index.ts#L1-L33), [testing export](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/common/plugin-runtime/sdk/src/testing/index.ts#L1-L3), [runtime interfaces](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/common/plugin-runtime/sdk/src/runtime.ts#L22-L66) |
| Contract or setting | [Package exports](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/common/plugin-runtime/sdk/package.json#L1-L16), [declaration build](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/common/plugin-runtime/sdk/tsconfig.build.json#L1-L21) |
| Test evidence | Compiler enumeration of both actual entrypoints is a documentation fact check; it does not execute plugin code. |
| Revision | `586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1` |
| Limit | Import visibility and type discovery do not prove module activation or a live plugin run. |

## From plugin request to adapter result

1. The registry validates a plugin manifest and its referenced schemas. A registration names the module, exported function, schemas and required capabilities. An adapter registration also declares the capabilities it provides.
2. The runtime supplies a `PluginContext` contract and wraps it in `PluginInvocationContext`. The contract carries a lease, resolved input/configuration and visible artifact references. Optional producer profiles remain explicit data.
3. A plugin calls `context.invoke(capability, request)`. The runtime checks its active lease, the capability grant and the capability's constraints. For a tagged runtime dispatch request, it also validates the closed invocation schema. The request's `resource.canonicalId` has capability-specific meaning; it is not always a relative path.
4. The adapter runtime chooses the granted provider. For an ordinary durable effect, the effect coordinator owns request identity, the journal, resource locking and receipts. A repeated idempotency key must still match the original request and payload. The adapter receives an `AdapterInvocation`, not an arbitrary unadmitted payload.
5. The adapter checks cancellation and, for a nonconfidential invocation, the current fence before the external effect. A **fence** rejects work whose lock is no longer current. The adapter returns a record; the coordinator determines durable completion and the runtime returns the completed result.

A typed object does not create permission. The SDK interfaces provide no authorization shortcut. A plugin's `emit` also requires an active lease, its own `plugin.<pluginId>.` event namespace and the current run ID. `artifact(id)` returns only a visible reference, or `undefined`; it also checks the lease.

This source-only stage fragment has no external effect. It returns the passed variant with the required empty artifact list:

```ts
import type { PluginInvocationContext, StageResult } from '@kubeclaw/plugin-sdk';

export async function execute(context: PluginInvocationContext): Promise<StageResult> {
  return { schemaVersion: 'stage-result.v2', outcome: 'passed', artifacts: [] };
}
```

The fragment alone does not register or activate the stage. A manifest, matching schemas and the runtime's supported execution route are still required. For other outcomes, use the [StageResult entry](sdk-exports.md#stageresult): failure/continuation variants have different required fields.

### Adapter lifecycle and cleanup

`AdapterFactory` can return an instance directly or through a Promise. Core starts required adapter dependencies first, calls the factory, then calls `ready()`. A dependency cycle, missing provider, factory rejection or readiness failure stops startup. Core rolls back started instances and aborts their lifecycle controllers. It bounds activation and readiness with the configured shutdown timeout.

Implement `ready`, `invoke` and `shutdown`. `receipt` is optional and can return `undefined` when the adapter cannot find an earlier external result. Do not turn an uncertain external outcome into a new effect merely because a caller rejected or lost its response.

In `AdapterInvocation`, `confidential: true` excludes `lock` and `fence`. The other variant requires both and permits absent or false `confidential`. Confidential invocation is a separate core path; it is not permission for an adapter to ignore its grants or cancellation.

`AdapterActivationContext.invoke` and `invokeConfidential` use dependency grants. `AdapterDependencyOptions.signal` joins the lifecycle and parent signals. A supplied `deliveryId` must be a nonblank string of at most 512 UTF-16 code units and requires a parent invocation. It is not an observer-delivery ID.

Optional `withCleanup` gives the current adapter invocation one bounded cleanup phase. It does not extend capability grants. Core rejects cleanup without a current parent owned by that adapter. Cleanup receives its own signal; lifecycle revocation still applies. Await cleanup and honor its signal. There is no SDK-wide promise that cleanup rolls back every external effect.

| Source field | Evidence |
| --- | --- |
| Claim | Runtime methods enforce lease/grant/namespace boundaries; adapter startup and dependency calls retain lifecycle, cancellation and cleanup ownership. |
| Implementation | [Plugin context methods](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/nova/core/execution/context.ts#L34-L80), [adapter dependency calls](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/nova/core/execution/adapter-startup.ts#L57-L98), [cleanup ownership](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/nova/core/execution/adapter-startup.ts#L127-L142), [adapter runtime result handling](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/nova/core/execution/adapters.ts#L47-L63) |
| Contract or setting | [SDK runtime methods and variants](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/common/plugin-runtime/sdk/src/runtime.ts#L22-L117), [effect identity comparison](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/nova/core/effects/identity.ts#L20-L48) |
| Test evidence | Source-only for this invocation sequence. No provider or external capability was called for this page. |
| Revision | `586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1` |
| Limit | These interfaces and source checks do not prove live timeout, rollback or exactly-once external execution. |

## TypeScript shapes and schema checks

The generated contracts come from the owning plugin-system v2 JSON Schema. The product generator compiles a wrapper called `PluginSystemV2` and appends the two frozen current profile constants. Its exported auxiliary `PluginManifest1` expresses the nonempty-registration alternatives; it is not a complete manifest by itself.

TypeScript cannot express every runtime constraint. A string alias such as `NamespacedId` does not check a pattern or maximum length. A number member does not enforce integer or minimum rules. Some generated open shapes use `any`; strict JSON serializers can reject values that those types accept. `EffectReceipt` needs conditional status rules, and `StageResult` needs outcome-specific fields. Read the schema rows and consumer explanation together.

The [inventory](sdk-exports.md) preserves recursive rules, including required arrays, enums/constants, patterns, bounds, references and conditional branches. Parent `required` arrays determine presence. Follow `$ref` to its target. An absent `default` means that the schema declares no default; it does not establish a fallback in a separate consumer.

Registry `parsePluginManifest` parses JSON and applies the manifest validator. `validateContractValue` validates a named canonical definition; its profile cases first apply strict profile parsing. Referenced schemas have separate `validate` and `resolve` paths: `validate` does not apply defaults, while `resolve` clones the input and uses its schema defaults. This is foundation behavior, not a validator automatically attached to each SDK type.

JSON Schema `minLength`/`maxLength` measure Unicode code points. Runtime string `.length` and `.slice` measure UTF-16 code units. For example, 512 emoji meet the `EffectDeliveryId` schema maximum of 512 code points but occupy 1024 code units and fail the adapter dependency runtime maximum. Result-file paths have a separate UTF-8 byte limit. Do not substitute one measurement for another.

Schema acceptance also does not prove a resource exists, a path is contained, a graph is acyclic or a producer is authorized. The component that owns that boundary must perform its additional checks.

| Source field | Evidence |
| --- | --- |
| Claim | Types are generated from schema; runtime validation and default resolution are separate operations. |
| Implementation | [Type generation](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/scripts/generate-plugin-sdk-types.mjs#L1-L33), [manifest validation](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/common/plugin-runtime/foundation/registry/schema.ts#L66-L86), [referenced schema resolution](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/common/plugin-runtime/foundation/registry/schema.ts#L90-L117), [canonical validation](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/common/plugin-runtime/foundation/registry/schema.ts#L133-L148) |
| Contract or setting | [Plugin-system schema definitions](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/common/plugin-runtime/contracts/plugin-system/v2/plugin-system-v2.schema.json#L5-L1178) and the narrow definition/field rows in the [inventory](sdk-exports.md) |
| Test evidence | Source-only for schema validation; no validator success is inferred from a TypeScript signature. |
| Revision | `586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1` |
