## JSON and digest rules

A **canonical JSON codec** gives one serialized text for the same accepted data under its ordering rules. The SDK has two codecs because existing stored identities must keep their original bytes.

`canonicalJson` is the legacy codec. It sorts object keys with JavaScript `localeCompare`; collation can differ between locales. Preserve this codec where an untagged historical identity uses it. `portableJson` sorts keys by UTF-16 code units with `<` and `>` comparisons. Its ordering is independent of locale. It is not a claim of compliance with an unrelated JSON canonicalization standard.

Both codecs accept:

- `null`, strings and booleans;
- finite numbers, with negative zero serialized as `0`;
- plain records with `Object.prototype` or null prototype;
- dense arrays with the standard `Array.prototype`;
- enumerable own data properties whose values remain inside this same domain.

They reject unsupported values instead of silently dropping or converting them. They do not call accessors, `toJSON` or proxy traps. Repeated references to the same object are permitted when they do not form a cycle. Object keys are sorted; array order stays unchanged. These serializers do not normalize Unicode.

| Rejection | Error |
| --- | --- |
| NaN, Infinity or negative Infinity | `CANONICAL_JSON_NUMBER_INVALID` |
| undefined, function, symbol, BigInt or another unsupported scalar | `CANONICAL_JSON_VALUE_UNSUPPORTED` |
| Proxy | `CANONICAL_JSON_PROXY_UNSUPPORTED` |
| A cycle on the current traversal path | `CANONICAL_JSON_CYCLE` |
| A nonplain object or nonstandard array prototype | `CANONICAL_JSON_PROTOTYPE_INVALID` |
| Symbol keys, hidden properties, accessors or missing/nonenumerable array entries | `CANONICAL_JSON_PROPERTY_INVALID` |
| Array keys/count inconsistent with a dense standard array, including extra entries | `CANONICAL_JSON_ARRAY_INVALID` |

The exact first error depends on the first failed check. A sparse array can fail the key-count check or the data-property check. Do not require one error for every malformed array.

### A safe local example

This complete example uses only pure SDK values. Use Node.js 24 and the existing workspace dependencies. Follow [Prepare a clean checkout](../extend/developer-handbook.md#prepare-a-clean-checkout) for the repository preparation route. Run this example from the prepared repository root. This pure-value exercise does not prove a fresh lockfile install or completion of the full developer bootstrap. Save it as `sdk-json-example.ts` inside that workspace so the private package can resolve. Run `node sdk-json-example.ts`, then remove only that example file when finished. It makes no capability call and writes no product state.

```ts
import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
import { canonicalJson, portableJson, sha256Text } from '@kubeclaw/plugin-sdk';

const value = { Z: 1, a: [null, true], 'é': 2 };
const portable = portableJson(value);
assert.equal(portable, '{"Z":1,"a":[null,true],"é":2}');
assert.equal(sha256Text(portable), sha256Text('{"Z":1,"a":[null,true],"é":2}'));
assert.throws(() => portableJson({ omitted: undefined }), /CANONICAL_JSON_VALUE_UNSUPPORTED/);
// The legacy ordering depends on the current locale. Do not relabel these bytes.
const legacy = canonicalJson(value);
console.log({ portable, legacy });
```

The expected portable string and rejection above are source-checked. This displayed example passed twice on Node.js 24.21.0 in an isolated local checkout with copied existing dependencies. Those runs do not prove a fresh locked installation or the full developer bootstrap. The existing SDK JSON regression below was also executed locally. The printed legacy string is deliberately not assigned a universal cross-locale expectation.

`sha256Text` hashes text as UTF-8. `sha256Bytes` hashes the exact `Uint8Array`. Both return `sha256:` followed by 64 lowercase hex digits. Neither helper selects a JSON codec, inserts a version marker or validates producer authority. Choose the bytes first. JavaScript string length is not UTF-8 byte size.

The legacy codec's source comment records the reason for retaining it: those bytes already identify durable artifacts and effect payloads. A portable ordering requires a separately versioned change. The cost is that historical identity verification can depend on the original locale-sensitive serialization. Do not claim that attaching a new tag repairs old evidence.

| Source field | Evidence |
| --- | --- |
| Claim | Both codecs enforce a strict JSON domain; only object-key ordering differs. Hashes apply to exact UTF-8 text or supplied bytes. |
| Implementation | [Codecs and rejection checks](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/common/plugin-runtime/sdk/src/values.ts#L34-L97), [hash helpers](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/common/plugin-runtime/sdk/src/values.ts#L99-L105) |
| Contract or setting | [Portable encoding token and key order](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/common/plugin-runtime/sdk/src/values.ts#L49-L54) |
| Test evidence | [SDK JSON regression](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/common/plugin-runtime/sdk/tests/values.test.ts#L1-L30): locally passed with `node skills/common/plugin-runtime/sdk/tests/values.test.ts`. |
| Revision | `586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1` |
| Limit | The bounded check covers JSON domain and expected bytes, not live artifact storage, all host locales or producer authorization. |

## Version and absence rules

A missing tag means the owner retains historical behavior. It does not mean “select the newest encoding.” Present `undefined` is not always equivalent to omission. For strict JSON serialization, `undefined` is invalid even before a profile parser compares its value.

| Contract | Explicit current selection | Absence and rejection |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Portable artifact/reference encoding | `encoding: 'kubeclaw-json.utf16.v1'` | An untagged artifact preserves original historical bytes. A different explicit encoding fails; never recanonicalize it and keep its old digest. |
| Review/source identity | `identityEncoding: 'kubeclaw-json.utf16.v1'` | For dense plain-JSON records, omission uses legacy `canonicalJson`; an own different value, including undefined, fails. Ordinary property lookup can select an inherited portable tag; an inherited unknown tag is not rejected and selects legacy. See the local-parser limits below. |
| Runtime dispatch profile | `{schemaVersion:'runtime-dispatch-profile.v1', encoding:'json-utf16-v1'}` | Historical absence preserves the legacy producer. Exact finite profile only; no inferred fallback. |
| Review cache profile | `{schemaVersion:'review-cache-profile.v1', recordVersion:'review-content-cache.v2', encoding:'json-utf16-v1'}` | Historical absence preserves review-content-cache.v1 and its original effect operations. Other transport/artifact versions imply nothing. |
| Run snapshots | v3 requires runtime profile; v4 requires runtime and cache profiles | Read the selected snapshot schema. An absent cache member in the v3 shape does not select the current cache producer. |

The artifact/identity token `kubeclaw-json.utf16.v1` and profile encoding value `json-utf16-v1` are different exact strings. Use the exported constants and current profile values at their own boundaries.

`parseRuntimeDispatchProfile` and `parseReviewCacheProfile` compare the complete portable serialization to their generated constants. Extra fields, missing fields or unknown versions fail. Strict JSON rejection can occur before the profile-specific error.

`withRuntimeDispatchProfile(request, undefined)` returns the original request without validating it. With a profile, it validates the profile and strict JSON request, rejects an already-owned top-level profile key, and returns a shallow frozen copy. It does not deep-freeze payload or check dispatch operation/resource.

`runtimeDispatchProfileFields` first validates strict JSON. Missing own profile key returns `{}`. If a profile is present and a supplied capability is not `runtime.dispatch`, it rejects. With the capability argument omitted, that capability check does not run. Core also validates the tagged invocation schema: operation must be `dispatch` and resource type must be `runtime.agent`. A nested payload key with the same name is ordinary data.

`reviewCacheProfileFromContext` validates strict JSON of the whole context before checking the own profile key. Missing key returns `undefined`. Present undefined, null or malformed data does not select historical absence.

| Source field | Evidence |
| --- | --- |
| Claim | Profiles are independent, explicit finite contracts; helper omission paths differ from present malformed values. |
| Implementation | [Runtime profile helpers](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/common/plugin-runtime/sdk/src/runtime-dispatch-profile.ts#L1-L30), [cache profile helpers](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/common/plugin-runtime/sdk/src/review-cache-profile.ts#L1-L17), [review identity selection](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/common/plugin-runtime/sdk/src/review-subject.ts#L22-L26) |
| Contract or setting | [Finite profiles and tagged invocation condition](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/common/plugin-runtime/contracts/plugin-system/v2/plugin-system-v2.schema.json#L6-L27), [run snapshot shapes](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/common/plugin-runtime/contracts/plugin-system/v2/plugin-system-v2.schema.json#L47-L67) |
| Test evidence | [Original snapshot/identity cases](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/tests/verification/reliability/source-snapshot-version.test.mjs#L20-L61) were inspected, not executed for this page. |
| Revision | `586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1` |
| Limit | A parser checks finite data; it does not establish a live producer, cache hit or transport completion. |

## Artifact-byte verification

`verifiedArtifactJsonText(response, expected)` verifies the producer's original text. Use a `get_json_bytes` response, not a response that contains only a parsed value. A parsed object alone cannot preserve the original byte order or whitespace.

The helper checks these boundaries in order:

1. Require `artifact-json-bytes.v1`, a string `jsonBytes`, its UTF-8 byte size and its SHA-256 digest to agree with the response.
2. Parse the original bytes. Compare portable serialization of the parsed value with `response.value`.
3. Compare the complete returned artifact reference with `expected`, including namespace, ID, media type, producer, digest, size and optional encoding. Also bind reference digest/size to response digest/size.
4. If encoding exists, require the portable tag and require the original bytes to equal portable serialization. If encoding is absent, retain the verified original historical text without recanonicalizing it.
5. Return the original `jsonBytes` string.

A self-consistent different producer reference is not equal to the expected reference even when both producers wrote identical bytes. This helper checks reference equality, not a general trust policy. It neither calls a capability nor validates the application's value schema. Invalid JSON parsing and strict JSON-domain errors also propagate.

`readBoundArtifact` adds the current run, `application/json` and a maximum size of 4,194,304 bytes before requesting `artifacts.read/get_json_bytes`. It verifies binding and returns the parsed value by a TypeScript cast. It does not guarantee that the value is a record at runtime. The source approval helpers then impose their own artifact namespace, ID, stage, attempt and subject rules.

This source-only call requires a real runtime context with the `artifacts.read` grant and an expected visible reference:

```ts
const value = await readBoundArtifact(expectedRef, context);
```

Do not substitute a model-generated reference or an arbitrary same-digest artifact. If binding fails, stop and inspect the expected reference and stored original bytes. Do not “repair” the digest by reserializing the parsed object. The artifact-store adapter selects exact references and preserves the historical codec on untagged writes; explicitly tagged writes use portable JSON.

| Source field | Evidence |
| --- | --- |
| Claim | Verification binds original bytes, parsed value and full expected reference without recanonicalizing untagged artifacts. |
| Implementation | [Byte verifier](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/common/plugin-runtime/sdk/src/artifact-json.ts#L1-L20), [bound artifact reader](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/common/plugin-runtime/sdk/src/source-approval.ts#L9-L19), [store byte read and codec selection](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/common/plugins/artifact-store/src/adapter.ts#L78-L114) |
| Contract or setting | [Artifact reference fields](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/common/plugin-runtime/contracts/plugin-system/v2/plugin-system-v2.schema.json#L564-L577) |
| Test evidence | [Exact reference and cross-locale artifact cases](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/tests/verification/reliability/sdk-json-portable.test.mts#L33-L79) were inspected, not executed for this page. |
| Revision | `586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1` |
| Limit | Pure byte checks are not proof of producer authorization, value-schema acceptance or live storage durability. |

## Source selection and approval

### Select an implementation revision

`resolveSourceRevision` requires exactly one defined selector: `revision` or `sourceStageId`. An explicit revision must be 40 or 64 lowercase hexadecimal characters. It causes no artifact read. The stage selector filters visible artifacts to the current run, selected stage, implementation namespace and `implementation:` ID prefix. It chooses the latest attempt and requires exactly one artifact at that attempt.

The helper reads exact bound bytes and requires `status: 'ready_for_testing'` with a valid `sourceRevision`. It does not fall back to ambient Git HEAD when a result is absent or ambiguous.

`resolveImplementationRevisions(stageId, context)` also selects the earliest attempt. Its `headBefore` becomes `base`; the latest committed candidate becomes `head`. With one artifact, the helper reads it once. Both values must match the revision grammar. This preserves the first implementation baseline across repairs.

### Capture and verify a review subject

`parseReviewSource` selects project, absolute repository root, architecture reference and unique safe relative paths. It sorts a copied path list. For dense plain-JSON input, text excludes NUL/CR/LF and has a 4096-UTF-16-code-unit maximum, and paths exclude absolute paths, backslashes and empty/dot/parent segments. There must be 1–128 paths. The parser returns only its declared source fields; it does not read Git.

A dense array has a value at every index. Here, plain-JSON input means data records without inherited contract fields, custom prototypes, getters or proxies. The local parser is not strict validation of arbitrary unknown objects. Required values can be inherited. Only an own `identityEncoding` is guarded; a truthy inherited tag is copied into an own output field, even when unknown. `Array.some` skips holes, so a one-hole paths array passes and becomes `[undefined]`. Require dense plain-JSON input before relying on the safe-path description. Getters and proxy traps can execute during local property reads. This is why the input precondition matters.

`captureReviewSubject` calls `git.repository.read` to freeze clean HEAD and the architecture reference. It reads the selected regular files at the architecture revision, verifies their returned path/mode/content digest/UTF-8 size, and binds current run plus input digest. It then verifies repository freshness again. The file content is architecture-revision content, not an implied copy of current HEAD.

`parseReviewSubject` verifies run/revision/digest fields and ordered file content. It checks the unsigned subject digest with the selected identity codec and returns the original subject. It does not contact Git. `verifyReviewSubject` adds clean-HEAD and architecture-reference checks. Optional `expectedSourceRevision` changes only the expected current source HEAD, not the architecture revision.

These helpers read repository data through a granted capability; they do not commit, modify source or approve a design. Capability errors propagate. A stale subject requires a newly selected/captured subject at the intended revisions, not a rewritten digest on old content.

### Require preflight and bound approval

A **source binding** names the selected preflight stage and input digest, with optional selected review stage and explicit identity codec. It is selection data, not an approval token. `parseSourceBinding` checks enumerable own string keys, not hidden/symbol keys or inherited properties, and returns the original reference. Its required fields can be inherited. Only an own encoding is guarded; an inherited unknown encoding can pass. A present `reviewStageId: undefined` also passes this parser, although it is outside strict JSON. Use trusted dense plain-JSON input and the owning contract; this helper is not a general unknown-input validator.

`sourcePreflight` selects the latest unique `source-preflight` artifact from the binding's stage and current run. It requires `source-preflight.v1` and `outcome: 'passed'`, parses the subject, and checks identity codec, input digest and returned contract digest. It returns the binding, subject and contract. Missing, failed or mismatched preflight is a rejection; an unrelated ancestor is not a fallback.

`approvedSource(context, expected)` uses that preflight. If its binding names a review stage, the matching report must pass and refer to the same subject. A report with findings needs a human approval that names the exact report digest/stage and same subject. With no expected binding, the helper searches the visible current-run architecture report/approval artifacts. If neither exists it returns `undefined`; an approval without a report fails. Multiple producer stages or tied latest attempts are ambiguous.

The helper then follows a bound blueprint synchronization result and implementation results for the same subject. A synchronization result for a different subject does not advance the revision. Matching synchronization must bind the old revision, repository and architecture. Implementation lineage must have one next edge per revision and no cycle. This function does not itself prove the current worktree equals the returned revision; use the caller's separate repository verification where its task requires it.

| Source field | Evidence |
| --- | --- |
| Claim | Source selection uses exact current-run artifacts; subject capture binds architecture file bytes; preflight and approval preserve explicit selection and subject lineage. |
| Implementation | [Revision selection](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/common/plugin-runtime/sdk/src/source-revision.ts#L6-L49), [source/subject parsing](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/common/plugin-runtime/sdk/src/review-subject.ts#L28-L49), [capture/freshness](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/common/plugin-runtime/sdk/src/review-subject.ts#L51-L84), [preflight](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/common/plugin-runtime/sdk/src/source-preflight.ts#L13-L39), [approval and lineage](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/common/plugin-runtime/sdk/src/source-approval.ts#L29-L96) |
| Contract or setting | `ReviewSource`, `ReviewSubject` and `SourceBinding` entries in the [inventory](sdk-exports.md) define the local source helper contract, separate from plugin-system generated types. |
| Test evidence | Source-only for approval/preflight/capture; artifact/identity regression sources were inspected, not run as a live approval workflow. |
| Revision | `586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1` |
| Limit | Correct artifact selection does not establish a universal trust decision, a commit operation or current repository freshness beyond checks the specific helper performs. |

## Workspace and agent-task helpers

`RuntimeWorkspaceReference` is the Git adapter's workspace record. `parseRuntimeWorkspace` requires its seven declared enumerable string keys, version, path/branch text bounds of 4096 UTF-16 code units, revision and exact canonical owner equality. It returns the original object. It does not normalize paths or verify repository containment, branch existence or the protected owner file. Those are adjacent runtime checks.

`runtimeWorkspaceGeneration(owner, scope)` hashes legacy canonical JSON of owner, repository root and branch, then returns only the 64 hex digits. `runtimeWorkspaceOwnerFile(workspacePath)` hashes that exact text into a relative owner-file name. Neither creates a workspace or reads/writes its owner record. Different textual paths can hash differently even when a filesystem resolves them to the same place. The hash applies to supplied UTF-8 bytes, without normalization. Distinct lone-surrogate strings can encode to the same replacement bytes and therefore have the same owner-file name. It is not a universal text-uniqueness guarantee.

`buildRuntimeAgentTask(payload, resultFile)` builds instruction text and serializes the payload once. It limits the result-file path to 2048 UTF-8 bytes and instructs atomic temporary-file/rename output. It does not write a file, validate path containment or validate the agent's response. The instructions state that runtime/core owns invocation identity and session evidence; the agent returns only declared result fields. The helper uses native `JSON.stringify`, not strict `portableJson`: undefined/function/symbol object fields are omitted, corresponding array entries become null, and nonfinite numbers become null. Enumerable getters and `toJSON` can execute during serialization and can transform the output or throw. Cycles and BigInt can throw. Use trusted, already-JSON data when these executable object effects are not intended. The instruction text does not freeze the original payload. JSON serialization errors propagate.

Source: [workspace helpers](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/common/plugin-runtime/sdk/src/runtime-workspace.ts#L4-L32), [agent-task text and path check](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/common/plugin-runtime/sdk/src/runtime-agent-task.ts#L1-L25). These statements are source-only and do not prove protected workspace admission or agent execution.

## Convenience values are not validators

| Helper | Selection and absence | Consequence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `selectDefinedValue` | First reader result other than null/undefined; otherwise last result; no readers returns undefined | false, zero and empty text are accepted. Later readers do not execute after selection. |
| `selectTruthyValue` | First truthy reader result; otherwise last result; no readers returns undefined | false, zero, empty text, NaN, null and undefined are skipped. |
| `selectPresentValue` | First nonempty string, otherwise empty text | Whitespace-only text is accepted; no trim occurs. |
| `arrayValue` | Input array by reference, otherwise a new empty array when native classification succeeds | No element validation; the generic type is a cast. Revoked proxies throw TypeError during `Array.isArray`. |
| `objectRecord` | Truthy non-array object by reference, otherwise a new empty object | Class instances/live proxies/accessors can pass; revoked proxies throw during native classification. Not strict JSON validation. |
| `textValue` | String unchanged, otherwise empty text | No conversion, trimming or parsing. |

Reader exceptions propagate from both reader-based selectors. `OptionalAbsenceReader<T>` is synchronous; it does not wait for an asynchronous absence check. `ValueRecord` is permissive and uses `any`; do not substitute it for a stronger owning contract.

`redactStructuredValue` replaces sensitive-key values with `[redacted]`, limits arrays/object entries to 1000. It retains a 65,536-UTF-16-code-unit text prefix and appends `[truncated]` (65,547 output units) and returns `[truncated]` beyond depth 16. The sensitive-key match includes authorization, cookie, password, secret, token, API-key and credential forms. This is a bounded presentation helper, not comprehensive secret detection. Object enumeration can execute getters or proxy traps. Do not pass hostile executable objects or assume a nonmatching value is safe to publish.

Source: [selectors and casts](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/common/plugin-runtime/sdk/src/values.ts#L4-L32).
[bounded redaction](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/common/plugin-runtime/sdk/src/values.ts#L107-L128). The exhaustive [inventory](sdk-exports.md) gives each helper's exact signature and source-only example.

## Test providers and report adapters

Test provider factories receive `ProviderInvocationV1` and return `TestProviderInstance`. `execute` receives the invocation plus `TestProviderExecutionContext` and resolves to `ProviderResultV1`; optional `cleanup` resolves to void. The context gives the cancellation signal, workspace root, output logging and granted capability invocation. Its request shape does not carry a runtime dispatch profile. Logging output is not the provider result. Read the [callback contract rules](sdk-exports.md#callback-contract-rules) for its nested fields and required/optional schema rules. Buster applies separate result admission checks: counts must sum to total, passed cannot contain failed tests. Evidence IDs and paths must be unique, and evidence types/report formats must match registration. A report must refer to declared test-report evidence; artifact outputs must refer to declared evidence IDs. The configured serialized result budget measures UTF-8 bytes. `providerDetails` uses the extra common E2E schema and exact schema digest only for `kubeclaw.playwright@1` or a details object naming `kubeclaw.e2e-result.v1`. Its case IDs and status totals must agree. The generated callback section gives the complete selected E2E fields and validators. These checks run in Buster, not in a TypeScript type.

The capability broker joins provider/call cancellation, limits pending requests and rejects duplicate request IDs. The SDK's context type does not grant arbitrary host access or prove that a declared `retrySafe` provider can repeat every external action. Register explicit evidence defaults and honor cleanup/cancellation in the actual provider.

A report adapter is a parser, not a capability adapter. `ReportAdapterInput` contains bytes, media type and case/finding limits; no invoke context is included. `ReportAdapterOutput` requires counts, duration, cases, findings and truncation/omitted-count fields from the test-gate contract. Buster adds adapter provenance and the source artifact; the callback must not manufacture those owner fields.

The [callback contract rules](sdk-exports.md#callback-contract-rules) contain the recursively resolved provider invocation, provider result and report result schemas, including nested input/output/evidence/finding/case fields. Read required arrays, union variants, bounds and referenced definitions together. These owning rules do not become SDK named exports.

After Buster adds provenance, report validation also requires outcome counts to sum to total and case IDs to be unique. For complete lists, omittedCaseCount must be zero and the list length and each outcome count must match the report totals. For truncated lists, omittedCaseCount must be positive, listed plus omitted must equal total, and listed outcomes cannot exceed exact totals. At report and case level, findingsTruncated must be true exactly when omittedFindingCount is positive. These are semantic validator rules, not TypeScript checks. Source: [report semantic validation](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/contracts/pipeline-test-gate/v1/src/validation.ts#L100-L145).

Buster verifies report media type, bytes, digest, package snapshot and result contract. It rejects output arrays above the selected count limits. `ReportAdapterLimits` has no SDK defaults and contains no process, memory or timeout fields; those belong to the wider report runtime configuration.

Source: [provider/report callback types](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/common/plugin-runtime/sdk/src/runtime.ts#L119-L184).
[provider capability broker](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/buster/engine/test-gates/provider-capabilities.ts#L3-L44).
[report input wrapper](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/buster/engine/test-gates/report-adapter-child.mjs#L21-L40).
[result binding and limits](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/buster/engine/test-gates/report-adapter-runtime.ts#L269-L320).
[required report fields](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/contracts/pipeline-test-gate/v1/src/types.ts#L342-L375). These are source-only statements; no provider or report child was started for this page.

## Testing entrypoint

`assertNever(value: never): never` is the only export from `@kubeclaw/plugin-sdk/testing`. Use it in the default branch of an exhaustive discriminated-union switch. It always throws `Unexpected contract variant: <JSON>`. It does not validate an unknown runtime value. If typing is bypassed, `JSON.stringify` can itself fail on cycles or BigInt before the intended error.

```ts
import { assertNever } from '@kubeclaw/plugin-sdk/testing';
type Choice = {kind:'yes'} | {kind:'no'};
function selected(choice: Choice): boolean {
  switch (choice.kind) {
    case 'yes': return true;
    case 'no': return false;
    default: return assertNever(choice);
  }
}
```

This is a source-only example, not a testing framework or live plugin harness. Source: [assertNever](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/586f1f558a673c4df51c0afb9ea535c814b933b1/skills/common/plugin-runtime/sdk/src/testing/index.ts#L1-L3).

## Error handling and retry boundary

| Error family | Meaning and next check |
| --- | --- |
| `CANONICAL_JSON_*` | Input is outside the strict JSON domain. Remove unsupported values only according to the owning contract; never silently drop evidence fields. |
| `RUNTIME_DISPATCH_PROFILE_INVALID`, `RUNTIME_DISPATCH_PROFILE_ALREADY_BOUND`, `RUNTIME_DISPATCH_PROFILE_CAPABILITY_INVALID` | Wrong finite profile, duplicate top-level binding or profile on another capability. Check explicit run selection and request metadata. |
| `REVIEW_CACHE_PROFILE_INVALID` | Present cache profile is not the exact current finite contract. Do not fall back to historical absence. |
| `ARTIFACT_JSON_BYTES_UNBOUND`, `ARTIFACT_JSON_VALUE_UNBOUND`, `ARTIFACT_JSON_REFERENCE_UNBOUND`, `ARTIFACT_JSON_ENCODING_INVALID` | Original bytes, parsed value, full expected reference or explicit encoding disagrees. Stop; inspect producer/reference/original bytes. |
| `SOURCE_IMPLEMENTATION_ARTIFACT_MISSING_OR_AMBIGUOUS`, `SOURCE_IMPLEMENTATION_ARTIFACT_CORRUPT`, `SOURCE_IMPLEMENTATION_REVISION_INVALID`, `SOURCE_IMPLEMENTATION_BASE_INVALID` | Selected implementation result is absent, tied, corrupt or has invalid head/base data. Inspect same-run selected-stage artifacts, not ambient HEAD. |
| `SOURCE_REVISION_SELECTION_INVALID`, `SOURCE_REVISION_INVALID` | Exactly-one selector or explicit revision grammar failed. Fix selection input before retry. |
| `REVIEW_IDENTITY_ENCODING_INVALID`, `REVIEW_SOURCE_INVALID`, `REVIEW_SUBJECT_INVALID`, `REVIEW_SUBJECT_FILE_INVALID`, `REVIEW_SUBJECT_DIGEST_INVALID` | Source/subject identity or file evidence failed. Check codec selection, ordered paths and exact file bytes. |
| `REVIEW_REPOSITORY_IDENTITY_INVALID`, `REVIEW_SOURCE_BYTES_INVALID`, `REVIEW_SUBJECT_STALE` | Git capability response or current revisions disagree. Recheck selected repository and references; capture a new subject when intended source changes. |
| `SOURCE_PREFLIGHT_BINDING_INVALID`, `SOURCE_PREFLIGHT_REQUIRED`, `SOURCE_PREFLIGHT_FAILED`, `SOURCE_PREFLIGHT_INPUT_MISMATCH` | Selected immutable preflight is not available, passed or bound to this input. Do not select an unrelated result. |
| `SOURCE_APPROVAL_ARTIFACT_INVALID`, `SOURCE_APPROVAL_ARTIFACT_CORRUPT`, `SOURCE_APPROVAL_AMBIGUOUS`, `SOURCE_APPROVAL_REQUIRED`, `SOURCE_APPROVAL_BINDING_INVALID` | Artifact admission/bytes, unique selection or exact report/approval binding failed. Preserve selected evidence and inspect the specific relationship. |
| `SOURCE_APPROVAL_SYNC_BINDING_INVALID`, `SOURCE_APPROVAL_IMPLEMENTATION_INVALID`, `SOURCE_APPROVAL_LINEAGE_AMBIGUOUS` | Source advancement is invalid, forked or cyclic. Resolve the actual lineage; do not choose an arbitrary edge. |
| `RUNTIME_WORKSPACE_REFERENCE_INVALID`, `RUNTIME_WORKSPACE_OWNER_MISMATCH` | Workspace shape/text/revision or attempt owner disagrees. This is not a signal to overwrite the protected owner record. |
| `RUNTIME_RESULT_FILE_PATH_TOO_LONG` | UTF-8 result path exceeds 2048 bytes. Select a shorter supported path; the helper has not written a file. |

Errors from reader callbacks, JSON parsing/stringifying, Node crypto or capability implementations can also propagate. Do not expect every rejection to have an SDK-prefixed code. Async helpers can reject after a read capability has run; a Promise rejection does not establish that every downstream operation had no effect.

Pure serialization and local parsing have no intended external mutation. Capability-backed reads can involve the runtime's own effect/journal path. The adapter invocation path can perform external effects. Before retrying an uncertain external outcome, inspect its original idempotency identity and receipt. A fresh key is a new effect, not recovery of the old one. Use the owning component's recovery procedure rather than a universal SDK retry loop.

## Maintain this reference

The canonical explanation is this page. Documentation tooling generates the export/field inventory; do not edit it by hand. Documentation tooling uses the actual package export map and TypeScript compiler, then joins separately authored consumer explanations with source dependencies. A new, removed or renamed export, a missing explanation or changed bound semantic source must fail the check. Hash equality is a drift guard, not proof of explanation quality.

When an API changes, review its caller, provider, validator, schema and tests. Update its consumer meaning, optional/absence behavior, errors, side effects and version limits together. Do not refresh a source digest without reading the affected behavior. The SDK's own type generator remains responsible for product contract declarations; the documentation inventory does not replace it or execute product modules.

Run the read-only reference check from the repository root:

```bash
npm run docs:sdk:check
```

Exit zero means that the inventory and export page match the current discovered surface and bound consumer explanations. A source or output drift error stops the check. Review the named source and its consumer behavior before updating the explanation or regenerating; do not edit the generated page to conceal drift. This check does not activate plugins or prove live capability behavior.

<!doctype html>
<html lang="en"><head><meta charset="utf-8"><link rel="canonical" href="/reference/documentation-governance.html"></head><body><main>
<h1 id="documentation-governance">Documentation Governance</h1>
<p>Status: current maintenance contract
Audience: documentation contributor, maintainer, reviewer
Owner: documentation
Evidence: <a href="https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/c559e97960a89a0f80487483addb91d2e6e4527d/scripts/docs-governance.mjs">scripts/docs-governance.mjs</a>; <a href="https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/c559e97960a89a0f80487483addb91d2e6e4527d/scripts/docs-publication.mjs">scripts/docs-publication.mjs</a>; <a href="https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/c559e97960a89a0f80487483addb91d2e6e4527d/scripts/docs-status.mjs">scripts/docs-status.mjs</a>; <a href="https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/c559e97960a89a0f80487483addb91d2e6e4527d/docs/status/open-issues.json">docs/status/open-issues.json</a>
Applies to: all reader-facing files below docs/site
Last verified: source-bound maintenance revision; publication and reader results remain separate</p>
<h2 id="purpose">Purpose</h2>
<p>This page explains which document owns each type of information. It also explains how to keep that information current.</p>
<p>A link can work while its statement is false. A generated table can be current while its explanation is unclear.
For these reasons, KubeClaw checks source facts and authored explanations through separate paths.</p>
<h2 id="one-canonical-home-for-each-topic">One Canonical Home for Each Topic</h2>
<p>Use the following homes. Link to a canonical page when another reader path needs the same explanation.
Do not copy the explanation into a second page.</p>
<table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Information</th>
<th>Canonical home</th>
<th>Owner role</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td>System behavior and component boundaries</td>
<td><code>understand/</code></td>
<td>The named component or platform owner</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Operator procedures and recovery</td>
<td><code>use/</code></td>
<td>Platform operations</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Extension contracts and development tasks</td>
<td><code>extend/</code></td>
<td>Plugin foundation or the named specialist owner</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Exact generated facts and shared terms</td>
<td><code>reference/</code></td>
<td>Documentation and the source component owner</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Enduring choices and their reasons</td>
<td><code>decisions/</code></td>
<td>Platform architecture or the named decision owner</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Current implementation limits</td>
<td><code>status/current.md</code> and <code>status/open-issues.md</code></td>
<td>Platform architecture and platform maintainers</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Planned work</td>
<td><code>status/roadmap.md</code></td>
<td>Platform maintainers</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Environment acceptance</td>
<td><code>status/acceptance.md</code></td>
<td>Platform operations</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<p>A page can link to several areas. It must not become a second authority for their facts.
For example, an operator procedure can summarize a trust boundary and link to its full architecture explanation.</p>
<h2 id="source-authority">Source Authority</h2>
<p>Use the narrowest source that owns a fact.</p>
<ol>
<li>A schema owns accepted fields, types, required values, and structural limits.</li>
<li>A runtime manifest owns package membership for that role.</li>
<li>Implementation source owns current behavior.</li>
<li>A focused test proves only the behavior that it executes.</li>
<li>A decision record owns an approved reason or constraint.</li>
<li>The open-issue register owns incomplete implementation work.</li>
<li>A live acceptance record owns results from a real environment.</li>
</ol>
<p>Do not use code as proof of approval. Do not use an accepted decision as proof of implementation.
Do not use a local test as proof of deployment behavior.</p>
<p>The publication tool checks page metadata and repository evidence paths. See the
<a href="https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/9509581d0e7bacfe36e4edf63618802753abbcd2/scripts/docs-publication.mjs#L831-L861">metadata check</a>.
The status generator reads the machine-owned issue register. See the
<a href="https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/9509581d0e7bacfe36e4edf63618802753abbcd2/scripts/docs-status.mjs#L113-L159">status source boundary</a>.</p>
<h2 id="generator-prerequisites">Generator prerequisites</h2>
<p>Run documentation generators from the repository root with Node.js 24 and
installed repository dependencies. API output discovery requires Helm
3.22.0 and Go 1.26.3 on <code>PATH</code>. Helm renders local charts against the declared
Kubernetes 1.35.0 API baseline; rendering does not deploy them. The Go helper
uses the standard parser to inspect the controller's source. It does not build
or run the controller. The documentation workflow
pins the same Helm and Go versions for both validation and trusted regeneration.
Use <code>node --version</code>, <code>helm version --short</code>, and <code>go version</code> to confirm the tools before generation.
If a required tool is absent, install it through the development environment's
approved toolchain procedure. A missing parser is a failed prerequisite, not
permission to omit controller-produced API objects from the inventory.</p>
<h2 id="authored-explanations-and-generated-facts">Authored Explanations and Generated Facts</h2>
<p>Authored text explains purpose, sequence, reasons, consequences, failure, and recovery.
A maintainer reviews this text when a source dependency changes.</p>
<p>Generated content lists repetitive facts from manifests, schemas, or registers.
Do not edit generated content by hand. Change its source or generator, and then regenerate it.</p>
<p>Use the <a href="generated-documentation-map.json">generated page map</a> to locate each
page's declared owner, content kind, source dependencies, and checks. Do not
maintain a second manual inventory here. The governance generator builds the map from reader-page
metadata; it does not prove complete source discovery or successful checks.
A missing or stale page entry must be corrected through its metadata and the
map generator before the change is called covered.</p>
<p>The plugin catalogue has two input authorities. Source manifests and schemas
own mechanical package facts. Maintainers own the purpose, use, exclusions, and
limits in the catalogue guidance input. The generator reads that guidance; do
not edit its generated package pages as a second authored authority. See the
<a href="https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/9509581d0e7bacfe36e4edf63618802753abbcd2/scripts/docs-publication.mjs#L255-L299">current page construction</a>.
The plugin-foundation owner maintains package guidance. Documentation and the
source component owner maintain the generated-fact route. For other families,
use the page's named owner and the
<a href="../extend/documentation-maintenance.html#choose-one-fact-authority-and-its-explanation">maintenance workflow</a>.</p>
<h2 id="required-page-metadata">Required Page Metadata</h2>
<p>Every Markdown page has six fields directly below its title.</p>
<table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Field</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
<th>Update trigger</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><code>Status</code></td>
<td>The page's present claim, not a completion slogan</td>
<td>Its implementation or decision state changes</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><code>Audience</code></td>
<td>The reader roles that need the page</td>
<td>A supported task or reader path changes</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><code>Owner</code></td>
<td>The role that reviews and maintains the page</td>
<td>Component responsibility changes</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><code>Evidence</code></td>
<td>Repository sources that support the page</td>
<td>A source moves, splits, or loses authority</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><code>Applies to</code></td>
<td>The versions, components, or scope of the claim</td>
<td>Compatibility or scope changes</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><code>Last verified</code></td>
<td>The last source or environment review and its limit</td>
<td>The page receives a new applicable check</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<p>The generated <a href="generated-documentation-map.json">documentation map</a> records these fields for every page.
It also assigns the checks that protect each page. The generator fails if any page lacks required metadata.</p>
<p>Run these commands after a documentation change:</p>
<pre><code class="language-bash">npm run docs:governance:generate
npm run docs:governance:check
npm run docs:publish-check
</code></pre>
<p>The first command updates the map. The second command detects drift without changing files.
The final command runs the complete local publication gate.</p>
<h2 id="decision-records">Decision Records</h2>
<p>A stable decision identifier never changes its meaning. A decision record includes these items when evidence exists:</p>
<ul>
<li>the problem and its constraints;</li>
<li>the selected approach and its scope;</li>
<li>alternatives that the source records;</li>
<li>the reason for the choice;</li>
<li>benefits, costs, and operational consequences;</li>
<li>approval status and provenance;</li>
<li>implementation and verification status; and</li>
<li>its successor or related decisions.</li>
</ul>
<p>If historical evidence does not name an alternative, date, or approver, say that it is unknown.
Do not invent history to make the record look complete.</p>
<p>An accepted decision can have partial implementation. A replaced decision remains in history and points to its successor.
The successor states which part it replaces. It also states which earlier guarantees remain valid.</p>
<h2 id="deprecation-redirect-and-supersession">Deprecation, Redirect, and Supersession</h2>
<p>Use these rules before changing a published route or term:</p>
<ol>
<li>Select the successor page or term.</li>
<li>State why the old item is no longer correct.</li>
<li>State the compatibility period when one exists.</li>
<li>Add a redirect before removing a published route.</li>
<li>Link the old decision to its successor when historical meaning matters.</li>
<li>Remove an alias only after its declared compatibility period ends.</li>
</ol>
<p>The repository has no earlier public-route manifest. Therefore, this documentation does not invent redirects for unknown historical URLs.
The <a href="documentation-route-registry.json">route registry</a> records declared redirects and deprecated terms.
The generated map establishes the current route baseline. Future route changes must preserve declared redirects in the registry.</p>
<p>A repository source path is not automatically a published route. Moving an internal record does not create a public redirect requirement.</p>
<h2 id="definition-of-complete">Definition of Complete</h2>
<p>A page is not complete because it exists or because a build passes. Completion requires all applicable results below:</p>
<ul>
<li>claims agree with their source and declared revision;</li>
<li>mechanisms, ownership, failures, recovery, and limits have sufficient detail;</li>
<li>significant choices include reasons and consequences;</li>
<li>a new technical reader can follow the explanation without chat history;</li>
<li>instructions contain prerequisites, observations, stop conditions, and recovery;</li>
<li>extension tasks produce the declared checked result;</li>
<li>links and evidence resolve in source and publication output;</li>
<li>an owner, source dependency, and check protect the page; and</li>
<li>source, local-test, deployment, and human-acceptance states remain distinct.</li>
</ul>
<p>The project controlled-language review applies ASD-STE100 principles. Formal
ASD-STE100 verification or certification is not an acceptance gate and is not
claimed by this documentation.</p>
<h2 id="change-procedure">Change Procedure</h2>
<ol>
<li>Identify the canonical page and its owner in the generated map.</li>
<li>Read each listed source dependency before changing a claim.</li>
<li>Update authored explanation and generated facts through their separate paths.</li>
<li>Update decision and status pages when authority or implementation state changes.</li>
<li>Refresh <code>Last verified</code> with the actual check scope.</li>
<li>Generate the documentation map.</li>
<li>Run the mapped checks and the complete publication gate.</li>
<li>Record checks that need a live environment as pending.</li>
</ol>
<p>Do not change <code>Last verified</code> after a formatting-only edit. Do not claim a live result from static implementation evidence.</p>
</main></body></html>

ACTUAL CURRENT PRODUCTION HTML extend/documentation-maintenance.html
<!doctype html>
<html lang="en"><head><meta charset="utf-8"><link rel="canonical" href="/extend/documentation-maintenance.html"></head><body><main>
<h1 id="maintain-documentation-from-source-changes">Maintain documentation from source changes</h1>
<p>Status: source-described maintainer workflow; each change needs its own check and reader results
Audience: developer, documentation maintainer, documentation agent
Owner: documentation
Evidence: <a href="https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/c559e97960a89a0f80487483addb91d2e6e4527d/scripts/docs-governance.mjs">scripts/docs-governance.mjs</a>; <a href="https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/c559e97960a89a0f80487483addb91d2e6e4527d/scripts/docs-generate.mjs">scripts/docs-generate.mjs</a>; <a href="https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/c559e97960a89a0f80487483addb91d2e6e4527d/scripts/docs-inventory.mjs">scripts/docs-inventory.mjs</a>; <a href="https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/c559e97960a89a0f80487483addb91d2e6e4527d/scripts/docs-configuration-inventory.mjs">scripts/docs-configuration-inventory.mjs</a>; <a href="https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/c559e97960a89a0f80487483addb91d2e6e4527d/scripts/check-configuration-cli-drift.mjs">scripts/check-configuration-cli-drift.mjs</a>; <a href="https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/c559e97960a89a0f80487483addb91d2e6e4527d/scripts/check-configuration-drift-mutations.mjs">scripts/check-configuration-drift-mutations.mjs</a>; <a href="https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/c559e97960a89a0f80487483addb91d2e6e4527d/scripts/docs-platform-surface-inventory.mjs">scripts/docs-platform-surface-inventory.mjs</a>; <a href="https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/c559e97960a89a0f80487483addb91d2e6e4527d/scripts/docs-publication.mjs">scripts/docs-publication.mjs</a>; <a href="https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/c559e97960a89a0f80487483addb91d2e6e4527d/scripts/docs-tree-boundary.mjs">scripts/docs-tree-boundary.mjs</a>; <a href="https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/c559e97960a89a0f80487483addb91d2e6e4527d/scripts/docs-parity-transition.mjs">scripts/docs-parity-transition.mjs</a>; <a href="https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/c559e97960a89a0f80487483addb91d2e6e4527d/docs/config/documentation-tree-classification.json">docs/config/documentation-tree-classification.json</a>; <a href="https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/c559e97960a89a0f80487483addb91d2e6e4527d/docs/config/documentation-tree-baseline.json">docs/config/documentation-tree-baseline.json</a>; <a href="https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/c559e97960a89a0f80487483addb91d2e6e4527d/@S@reference/documentation-route-registry.json">@S@reference/documentation-route-registry.json</a>; <a href="https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/c559e97960a89a0f80487483addb91d2e6e4527d/package.json">package.json</a>; <a href="https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/c559e97960a89a0f80487483addb91d2e6e4527d/.github/workflows/docs-checks.yaml">.github/workflows/docs-checks.yaml</a>
Applies to: authored reader pages, source-derived documentation, and their ownership and publication inputs
Last verified: implementation behavior checked on 2026-10-15; no generation, check, mutation test, or reader exercise asserted</p>
<h2 id="start-with-the-changed-behavior">Start with the changed behavior</h2>
<p>A nested configuration field changes its default. Updating a table is not enough:
the consumer may apply a different default, an operator may need a restart, and
an old example may now request the wrong behavior. This workflow connects the
source change to its explanation, generated facts, failure path, and reader task.
Use it for a new surface, a changed meaning, and a removal. A documentation agent
uses the same steps and stop conditions as a human maintainer.</p>
<p>Use the <a href="developer-handbook.html#route-each-source-change-to-its-documentation-owner">Developer handbook</a>
for component-specific owners and checks. Use <a href="../reference/documentation-governance.html">documentation governance</a>
for canonical homes and source authority. This page joins those rules into one
change procedure; it does not replace the component's technical guide.</p>
<h2 id="prepare-a-bounded-change">Prepare a bounded change</h2>
<ol>
<li>Start from a checkout whose revision and existing changes you can identify.
Record the changed source paths, selected tools, dependency state, and intended
outputs. Preserve unrelated work. Use the handbook's
<a href="developer-handbook.html">checkout and component preparation</a> before running tools.</li>
<li>Name the reader's question and the behavior owner. Distinguish product source,
a supported dependency configuration, a generated fact, and explanatory prose.
A schema declaration alone does not prove a consumer inserts its default.</li>
<li>Open the <a href="../reference/generated-documentation-map.json">page map</a>. Locate the
source dependency or parent component and its owner, page, and declared checks.
Read the actual generator's boundary and the actual consumer. If no mapping
exists, assign an owner and canonical page before calling the change covered.</li>
<li>Identify permissions and effects before execution. Node.js 24 and locked root
dependencies are common prerequisites, not the whole toolchain. Follow the
<a href="developer-handbook.html#add-tools-and-credentials-only-for-the-selected-route">selected-route tools</a>
for any Helm, Go, Kubernetes, native, or other requirement. A source or local
check is not a live deployment result. Missing prerequisites stop that route;
do not switch to a partial parser or omit a source to obtain a green result.</li>
</ol>
<p>Keep a small change record: source boundary, parser/selector, owner, canonical
page, generator or manual explanation, check, reader task, and exclusions.
This record is a work aid. Registering it does not prove discovery complete.
The generated page map lists metadata dependencies and check names, but does not
itself inventory every public surface, run a reader task, or choose every test.</p>
<h2 id="discover-the-whole-affected-family">Discover the whole affected family</h2>
<p>Search the source boundary before selecting a convenient example. Include
conditional and disabled supported branches, shared helpers, generated names,
and callers that change defaults or authority. Read the actual extractor's
roots, exclusions, and supported syntax. An unsupported source form is a blocker
for that extractor, not proof that the surface does not exist.</p>
<table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Family</th>
<th>Facts to preserve with a source location</th>
<th>Reader destination and bounded tool route</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td>Schema and configuration</td>
<td>Recursive objects, arrays, alternatives, conditions, required relationships, constraints, schema defaults versus runtime defaults, deprecated fields, consumer, precedence, sensitive data, reload/new-run effects</td>
<td>Start at <a href="../reference/configuration-precedence.html">Configuration precedence</a>, the selected family page, and <a href="../reference/plugin-configuration.html">Plugin configuration</a>. <code>docs:inventory:config</code> derives its declared YAML/schema/runtime-input inventories; inspect its boundary before treating them as global coverage.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Commands and flags</td>
<td>Owning entrypoint, subcommand, aliases, value-taking, required/optional relationships, defaults, duplicate and unknown-option behavior, exit/failure meaning, consumer</td>
<td>Use <a href="../reference/cli.html">CLI commands</a> and the component guide. <code>docs:inventory</code> covers deployment/secret/Helm/workflow facts; the configuration runtime-input inventory has a separate owned-definition CLI surface. <code>docs:drift:cli:check</code> checks that inventory and its declared semantics. External-command invocations are not owned CLI definitions.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Contract</td>
<td>Exact identity/version, producer and consumer, schema/type relationship, compatibility direction, migration and retirement</td>
<td>Use <a href="../reference/configuration-compatibility.html">Configuration compatibility</a>, <a href="../reference/sdk.html">SDK</a>, or the component's contract guide. Select its real generator/check; do not assume a configuration-schema extractor covers all request, result, and snapshot contracts.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Event</td>
<td>Producer, consumer or explicit unknown consumer, payload/version, transport, correlation, ordering, repeat delivery, persistence and retention</td>
<td>Use <a href="../understand/telemetry.html">Telemetry</a> and the component guide. The <a href="../reference/platform-surfaces-generated.html">platform surface reference</a> supplies bounded facts, not every payload's meaning. Read its discovery boundary.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Error</td>
<td>Exact code or dynamic construction rule, throw/emit location, trigger, effect and lifecycle state, retry rule, safe action, retained data</td>
<td>Use <a href="../reference/configuration-errors.html">Configuration errors</a>, <a href="../reference/buster-error-codes.html">Buster errors</a>, and the producing component. Follow dynamic prefixes/arguments and shared error constructors; a string-literal search alone cannot establish completeness.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Endpoint</td>
<td>Producer/listener, caller, Service/route/port, transport, authentication and authority, limits, failure and recovery</td>
<td>Use <a href="../reference/endpoints.html">Endpoints</a> and <a href="../reference/service-routing.html">Service routes</a>. Reconcile chart/config/runtime consumers, not only one default render.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Store</td>
<td>Writer/reader, exact path or resolution rule, authority, lifetime, retention, quota, cleanup, backup/restore and loss impact</td>
<td>Use <a href="../understand/data-and-state.html">Data and state</a>, <a href="../use/recovery.html">Recovery</a>, and <a href="../use/capacity.html">Capacity</a>. An absent default can defer to another authority; do not invent a retention policy or restore command.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Capability</td>
<td>Namespace, provider, caller grant, resource match, translation boundary, denial behavior</td>
<td>Use <a href="../reference/capabilities.html">Capabilities</a> and <a href="../reference/roles-and-package-ownership.html">Roles and package ownership</a>. A runtime role, Nova grant, Buster capability, and Kubernetes RBAC rule are not interchangeable.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Workflow</td>
<td>Trigger, owner, inputs/secrets, permissions, tools, outputs, external effects, success/failure and rollback limits</td>
<td>Use <a href="../reference/workflows.html">Workflow inventory</a> and the task procedure. <code>docs:inventory</code> derives declared workflow facts; an inventory of commands does not prove that they ran.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<p>For each affected family, compare discovered identities to documented identities
in both directions. Find a source with no page and a page with no current source.
Keep exclusions and unknown semantics visible. Do not extrapolate a fixed count,
one passing example, or one component's extractor to the whole product.
The product boundary includes KubeClaw-owned interfaces and supported dependency
choices; it does not require copying every unused upstream Kubernetes field.
Link applicable upstream definitions while explaining the choice KubeClaw makes.</p>
<h2 id="choose-one-fact-authority-and-its-explanation">Choose one fact authority and its explanation</h2>
<p>Put a mechanical fact in its source and let its owning generator derive it.
Write the reason, operational consequence, compatibility, and safe recovery in
the authored page. Do not manually maintain a second list of generated fields.</p>
<table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Change</th>
<th>Update together</th>
<th>Do not substitute</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td>Nested field/default</td>
<td>Consumer/schema relationship, recursive reference, effective-value explanation, example, reload/change impact, rejection case</td>
<td>A schema default for evidence that runtime inserts it</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>New flag</td>
<td>Actual parser, owner/subcommand, help/default/precedence/duplicate semantics, guide, negative input</td>
<td>A shell invocation of another tool for a new product option</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Changed error</td>
<td>Producer and dynamic-family rule, exact reference, lifecycle/retry/safe action, positive and negative case</td>
<td>A renamed label while keeping the old recovery instruction</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>New event or store</td>
<td>Producer/consumer or writer/reader, version/path resolution, authority, retention/failure explanation</td>
<td>A generated name list for operational meaning</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>New reader page</td>
<td>Six metadata fields, canonical classification, incoming task link, evidence paths, generated map inputs</td>
<td>A hand-written entry in the generated map</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<p>Use Status, Audience, Owner, Evidence, Applies to, and Last verified on every
reader page. Evidence paths are repository-relative and separated by semicolons.
Do not advance Last verified beyond the actual source or execution evidence.
Use a full committed revision and valid narrow line range for explicit pinned
source links. Compare the cited bytes to the current source; an unchanged old
link can point to behavior that no longer applies. Keep uncommitted source
provenance explicit until the publication revision can genuinely contain it.</p>
<p>A new page under <code>docs/site</code> also needs one explicit canonical record in
<code>docs/config/documentation-tree-classification.json</code>: matching actual and
expected path, canonical-reader class, purpose, original path, and the correct
introduced-after-baseline flag. Preserve the immutable baseline; do not recapture
it to conceal a new or moved file. The generic tree tooling and publication
selector must agree. Non-Markdown reader assets need their separate allowance.</p>
<h2 id="generate-only-owned-facts-then-check">Generate only owned facts, then check</h2>
<p>Follow the handbook's <a href="developer-handbook.html#documentation-change-chain">single common chain</a>.
The following choices explain its effects; they do not authorize a command in
an environment whose source/tool prerequisites have not been qualified.</p>
<table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Input change</th>
<th>Selected command and output</th>
<th>Check or failure meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td>Page metadata or evidence dependencies</td>
<td><code>npm run docs:governance:generate</code> writes <code>@S@reference/generated-documentation-map.json</code></td>
<td><code>docs:governance:check</code> compares expected bytes and validates route metadata. Missing owner/evidence needs source metadata repair, not a map patch.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Deployment/secret setup/Helm/workflow source facts</td>
<td><code>npm run docs:inventory</code> writes its four inventories under <code>docs/generated/inventory</code></td>
<td><code>docs:inventory:check</code> compares those expected JSON bytes. This does not cover every runtime input.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Declared configuration source families</td>
<td><code>npm run docs:inventory:config</code> writes values, schema, and runtime-input JSON inventories; then selected reference generation</td>
<td><code>docs:inventory:config:check</code> rejects stale bytes and verifies external Helm authorities on its successful-current path. Full discovery may acquire tools/children; it is not just a JSON comparison.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Source-derived reference sections</td>
<td><code>npm run docs:generate</code> updates its owned reference outputs</td>
<td><code>docs:generate:check</code> detects mismatch. Prepare the actual source revision and selected discovery route; do not suppress unsupported inputs.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Plugin catalogue/capability/operator matrix</td>
<td><code>npm run docs:publication:generate</code> writes catalogue/capability pages and the marked operator matrix</td>
<td><code>docs:publication:check</code> first runs helper tests and then compares/checks publication inputs. It does not validate every product example or live effect.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>New/moved documentation inputs</td>
<td><code>npm run docs:tree:generate</code> derives the tree inventory from classification, baseline, current inputs, and consumers</td>
<td><code>docs:tree:check</code> rebuilds and compares the model. Its dependency/consumer analysis has prerequisites; do not infer a pure file read from its name.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>Source — ownership records and generator selection</strong></p>
<p>Claim: page metadata supplies ownership/dependencies; generators select their owned outputs; check modes compare the derived result instead of making an authored page authoritative product input.
Implementation: <code>scripts/docs-governance.mjs</code>, <code>pages</code> metadata and generator selection, lines 85–108; output/check/generate branch, lines 124–143. <code>scripts/docs-generate.mjs</code>, <code>stableWriteMap</code>, lines 131–154, selects reference outputs. <code>scripts/docs-tree-boundary.mjs</code>, <code>main</code>, lines 904–936, selects byte comparison or tree output writing.
Contract: the six metadata fields and source dependencies feed the generated page map; classification and the immutable baseline feed the tree model. A map row or selected output is not evidence of complete source discovery.
Validation: no generator, checker, mutation test, or reader task execution for this statement.
Checkout identity, SHA-256: governance <code>a4a44fdb62fb8a963e1e7aa84fdacf41edfab06282e31938bda412252ea18502</code>; reference generator <code>c83c5653177b9b0a56326b0e315c409dc3c814c95d37dd53933402a2288aa718</code>; tree checker <code>a432a90ab447e4f9b944fae829aca7841eaad61493bc59fba629d7d2c3610a92</code>. These identify current file bytes, not claimed committed revisions.
Limit: selected SDK/discovery, source/Git and child-process prerequisites remain in force. The locators do not admit those effects or prove any generated result current.</p>
</blockquote>
<p>Inspect every generated diff and require exit zero before the next step.
Generators can write earlier files before a later failure. Retain the first
error, partial diff, selected revision and tool versions; fix the input or tool
cause and rerun that same owned generator. Do not use a broad reset, edit generated
facts manually, or pass a gap-allowance switch to hide a production omission.
For prose-only changes, avoid unrelated generator writes.</p>
<p>The common stable sequence is:</p>
<pre><code class="language-sh">set -eu
npm run docs:governance:check
npm run docs:generate:check
npm run docs:check:refs
npm run docs:site-boundary:check
npm run docs:publication:check
</code></pre>
<p>Run the changed component's named checks before publication checking. Keep the
actual command, exit status, cases passed/failed/skipped, tool versions, source
revision, and changed-file observations. Stop on the first failed prerequisite
or required check; do not average a failed boundary into other successes.
A command listed in the map is a requirement to evaluate, not a saved result.
The broader <code>docs:check</code> and <code>docs:publish-check</code> include additional tests and
staging effects. Use <a href="../reference/documentation-site.html">Documentation site and publication</a>
to inspect their output and distinguish Markdown staging from rendered behavior.</p>
<h2 id="prove-the-changed-meaning-not-a-fixed-count">Prove the changed meaning, not a fixed count</h2>
<p>Choose the smallest complete checks for the affected source and consumer.
For a nested default change, verify the actual absent-input result, an explicit
accepted value, an invalid value, and any conditional branch that changes the
default or authority. For a removed error identity, verify its absence from
current output, the retained dynamic family, and the changed safe-action text.
Do not mutate production source as an experiment while another task uses it.</p>
<p>Use isolated fixtures for addition, semantic change, and removal in each affected
reference family. First demonstrate a healthy baseline. Introduce one mutation,
require the intended drift failure and named affected page, restore the fixture,
and require recovery. A generic parser crash or unrelated stale file is not the
intended rejection. New-source and passive-neighbor cases distinguish discovery
from a hard-coded expected count. Preserve current bytes and the fixture diff.
The existing configuration mutation suite creates a temporary tree, copies
inputs, and starts generator/publisher child processes; it also queries Git.
It is not a no-write check. Inspect its current scope and dependencies before
selecting it, and do not apply its results to untested error/event/store families.</p>
<p>Validate examples against their actual contract, not merely JSON syntax.
Record the schema/version, consumer, setup, expected output, failure/cleanup,
and any external effects. Execute only in the documented isolated or authorized
environment. The publication checker has selected plugin and resume-signal
schema examples; that does not validate every operator or infrastructure command.
A compiled example is not evidence of activation, recovery, or a live service.</p>
<p>Ask independent readers to use the page without chat history or undocumented
maintainer hints. Give a concrete Find, Understand, and Act task chosen before
they read it. For the default example: find the field, explain which consumer
wins, identify restart/new-run impact, choose the valid override and safe
rejection response. Retain missing steps as defects. Independently check source
accuracy and language/navigation quality; the author cannot supply all of those
independent observations by rereading the same draft.</p>
<h2 id="rename-retire-and-recover">Rename, retire, and recover</h2>
<p>Before removing a page or source identity, account for every still-valid claim,
consumer, generated row, example, incoming link, owner, and historical route.
Move required information into its canonical successor and verify parity before
deleting the old authority. Use the <a href="../reference/documentation-route-registry.json">route registry</a>
and <a href="../reference/documentation-governance.html">governance</a> for a genuine public
rename, not for a fabricated former URL. Preserve fragment targets or explicitly
migrate them. The registry's structural validation does not prove server or
static redirects; test real old/new requests and anchors in the selected renderer.
The repository's transition tooling has its own manifests, source snapshots,
child checks and effects. Read that contract before a destructive transition;
an unrelated prose change does not require or permit a retirement run.</p>
<table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Failure</th>
<th>Stop and retain</th>
<th>Safe next action</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td>Source revision differs from current evidence</td>
<td>Source bytes, selected revision, first diagnostic</td>
<td>Complete the handbook's source-revision procedure; do not weaken a pin or cite an old commit as current.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>New identity lacks semantics or owner</td>
<td>The discovered source and unmapped identity</td>
<td>Assign its real consumer/owner and explanation; keep its missing coverage explicit.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Generator failed after writing</td>
<td>Exit status, first error, owned partial diff</td>
<td>Repair prerequisites/source, rerun only the responsible generator, and inspect unrelated work remains intact.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Check reports stale output</td>
<td>Exact path, expected owning generator, input revision</td>
<td>Repair input and regenerate that output. Never hand-edit it merely to satisfy equality.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Reader cannot finish a documented step</td>
<td>Task, failed step, missing prerequisite or unsafe ambiguity</td>
<td>Correct the guide and repeat the affected task with an independent reader.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Rename loses route/fragment or required meaning</td>
<td>Old link and successor mapping, parity defect</td>
<td>Restore the needed authority or fix successor/redirect before removal.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Local checks pass but live route is unavailable</td>
<td>Actual local results and the missing environment/prerequisite</td>
<td>Keep live behavior unverified; do not claim deployment, recovery, or retirement complete.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<p>Finish by reconciling source, authored explanation, generated facts, incoming
navigation, revision links, and the actual check/reader outcomes. Keep unresolved
families and live limitations visible. Publication is ready only for the scope
actually demonstrated; a complete-looking table is not proof of completeness.</p>
</main></body></html>

ACTUAL CURRENT PRODUCTION HTML extend/plugin-catalogue/README.html
<!doctype html>
<html lang="en"><head><meta charset="utf-8"><link rel="canonical" href="/extend/plugin-catalogue/README.html"></head><body><main>
<h1 id="plugin-catalogue">Plugin Catalogue</h1>
<p>Status: implemented
Audience: plugin author, operator, maintainer
Owner: plugin-foundation
Evidence: <a href="https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/c559e97960a89a0f80487483addb91d2e6e4527d/scripts/docs-publication.mjs">scripts/docs-publication.mjs</a>
Applies to: pipeline-plugin-v2, OpenClaw extensions, Codex plugins
Last verified: generated source listing only; package checks and their limits are recorded on each package page</p>
<h2 id="purpose">Purpose</h2>
<p>Use this catalogue to find every installable extension package and its declared surfaces.</p>
<p>The catalogue contains 51 packages.</p>
<p>Each package page combines two separate authorities. Maintainers write the practical
guidance separately from generated facts. The publication generator
reads manifests, schemas, role inclusion, and tests for mechanical facts. A generated
refresh cannot replace the authored purpose, use, exclusion, or limit with generic prose.</p>
<p>The plugin-foundation owner maintains purpose, use, exclusion, and limit text in
<code>docs/blueprint/AP08-catalogue-guidance.json</code>. The source component owner maintains
the manifest and schema facts. Do not edit generated catalogue pages as a second
authority. Change the owning input, then regenerate and run the declared checks.</p>
<h2 id="stage-packages">Stage Packages</h2>
<ul>
<li><a href="kubeclaw.architecture-validator.html">kubeclaw.architecture-validator</a></li>
<li><a href="kubeclaw.blueprint-sync.html">kubeclaw.blueprint-sync</a></li>
<li><a href="kubeclaw.buster-quality-gate.html">kubeclaw.buster-quality-gate</a></li>
